## `src/store/settings.ts` (2310 lines)

### S1 · `settings.ts:1596–2014` · `applySaved` is 418 lines of hand-written validation — **still present**

193 `parsed.` touches; ~26 of them are migration branches (e.g. `:1631` `blurBars` →
`blurMiniPlayer`, `:1639` `maxBitRate` → `maxBitRateCellular`, the `showQueueButton`/
`showDevicesButton`/`showSpeedButton` blob keys at `:1609–1620`).

The file already derives the per-key table it needs: `autoSetters` (`:1546–1560`)
builds one setter per `PERSISTED_KEYS`, and `applySaved` already uses the table for
the boolean subset (`:1622–1629`, `flags`). What is missing is the same table for the
non-boolean kinds. Cleaner shape — declarative validators next to `DEFAULTS`:

```ts
const VALIDATE: Record<PersistedKey, (v: unknown) => undefined | [key: Key, value: unknown]> = {
  maxBitRate:         numberFinite,
  downloadConcurrency: oneOf(DOWNLOAD_CONCURRENCY_OPTIONS),
  animatedArtworkFps: oneOf(ANIMATED_ARTWORK_FPS_OPTIONS),
  animatedArtworkUrls: cappedStringMap(500),
  backgroundTint:     oneOf(BACKGROUND_TINTS),
  appFont:            appFontValidator,
  themeMode:          isThemePreference,
  // …
};
const MIGRATE: Array<(parsed) => Partial<SettingsState>> = [
  migrateBlurBars, migrateBitrateCellular, migrateHomeChips, /* …26 named fns */
];
function applySaved(raw, set) {
  const parsed = coerceObject(raw);
  set(pickBooleans(parsed));                       // already table-driven
  set(Object.fromEntries(Object.entries(parsed)
        .flatMap(([k, v]) => VALIDATE[k]?.(v) ?? [])));
  for (const m of MIGRATE) set(m(parsed));
}
```
Impact ~250 lines, and the 26 migrations become individually nameable/testable —
which matters because `applySaved` is shared by the startup read (`:2243`) *and*
imported-file path (`:2208` with `customFontFamily/customFontUri` nulled by hand).

### S2 · four descriptions of the same settings — *still present*

`DEFAULTS` (`:1365`), `PERSISTED_KEYS` (`:1546`, derived from `DEFAULTS` — good),
`snapshot` (`:1357`), `exportedSettings` (`:2300`, `{...snapshot}` then two `delete`s).
The one that is *not* derived is the type-level one: `Persisted`/`PersistedKey`/`Reshaped`
plus the 22-line anonymous intersection at `:1600–1621` that re-lists the migrated keys.
A single `describeSettings()` producing `{ default, persisted: bool, validate, migrate }`
would let `snapshot`, `exportedSettings`, `autoSetters` and `applySaved` all read the
same table; impact folds into S1.

### S3 · `settings.ts:2208` — `applySaved({ ...(saved as object), customFontFamily: null, customFontUri: null }, set)` — *still present*

Nulling two keys to erase them works only because `applySaved`'s validators reject
`null` for those fields (`parsed.customFontUri` at `:1996` is `if (parsed.customFontUri)`).
That coupling is undocumented: change a validator to accept `null` (the type is
`string | null` in `DEFAULTS`, `:1409` region) and the export path silently starts
importing another phone's font. Pass an omission, not a `null`:
`applySaved({ ...saved, ...(omit keys) })` or an explicit `applySaved(raw, set, omit: Set<Key>)`.
Impact ~10 lines, severity: latent bug.

## `src/api/data.ts` (2125 lines)

### A1 · multi-library dispatch ladder hand-written 11× — **still present** (line numbers re-verified)

The same three-part preamble at `:375` (`getArtists`), `:487` (`getAlbumsByGenre`),
`:647` (`getSongList`), `:806`, `:889` (`getAppearsOn`), `:921` (`getMostPlayedSongs`),
`:950` (`getRandomSongs`), `:1212` (`getStarred`), `:1532` (`searchAlbums`),
`:1544` (`searchSongs`), `:1555` (`search`) — each is

```ts
if (!ids) …single default…;
if (ids.length === 1) return Subsonic.x(a, …, ids[0]);
return Promise.all(ids.map((id) => Subsonic.x(a, …, id))).then((parts) => /* ad-hoc merge */);
```
The only real variation is the merge rule (flat dedupe / `{artists,albums,songs}` split /
sort-by-`playCount` / shuffle). Cleaner shape:

```ts
const perLibrary = (fetchOne: (lib) => T[], merge: Merge<T> = dedupeById) =>
  async (ids, …) => ids.length <= 1 ? await fetchOne(only(ids)) : merge(await Promise.all(…));

const getStarredAcrossLibraries = perLibrary((id) => Subsonic.getStarred(a, id), mergeStarred);
```
`mergeStarred`, `mergeSearch`, `mergeRankedByPlayCount`, `mergeShuffled` become named,
reviewable functions. Impact ~60 lines deleted, and the merge rules stop being
`.then` bodies nobody can compare.

### A2 · two prefetch schedulers with two parallel counter sets — **still present**

`data.ts:1001–1026` (albums: `prefetchingAlbums` `:1001`, `lastAlbumPrefetch` `:1002`,
`ALBUM_PREFETCH_PER_RUN = 8` `:1003`) and `data.ts:1031–1099` (playlists:
`prefetchingPlaylists` `:1031`, `lastPlaylistPrefetch` `:1039`, `PREFETCH_PER_RUN = 5`
`:1047`) implement the identical background scheduler —
`if (busy) return; if (now - appStartedAt < PREFETCH_QUIET_MS) return;
if (now - last < PREFETCH_COOLDOWN_MS) return; busy = true; try { batches of CONCURRENCY }
finally { busy = false }` — with separate module flags.

Worse, the shared constants are interleaved *between* the two schedulers:
`PREFETCH_COOLDOWN_MS` (`:1038`) and `PREFETCH_QUIET_MS` (`:1050`) are declared *after*
the album scheduler that uses them (`:1007`, `:1008`). That is legal (`const` at module
scope, read only at call time) but it means the album scheduler's own body references a
constant the reader has to find 30 lines below a different function — and it is exactly
the shape that makes "which of these constants governs which scheduler" unanswerable in
review. Cleaner shape:

```ts
function schedulePrefetch(kind: 'albums' | 'playlists', whatIsStale: (window) => T[],
                          perRun: number, concurrency = 4) { … }
```
Impact ~70 lines; the two module counter pairs vanish with it.

### A3 · four genre endpoints have no offline branch — **still present** (prior doc's lines were 9 lower)

Verified: `data.ts:394 getGenres`, `:438 getAlbumsByGenre`, `:475 getGenreArt`,
`:764 getSongsByGenre` have no `isOffline()` / `serverOffline()` branch, while every
sibling list in the layer does (`getAlbumList`, `getArtists`, `getSongList`,
`getStarred`, `getPlaylists`). Offline the genre screen answers "Couldn't load genres"
plus a retry button instead of the local catalog's tags; the only thing keeping it out
of reach is the Home chip filter (`index.tsx:730 OFFLINE_KEYS`), which is a UI
coincidence, not a contract. Fix either way: add the branch (`localQueries` has the
tags) or state the online-only contract in the doc comment — the layer's own folder-nav
header at `:815` shows that pattern. Impact ~60 lines added, but it removes a whole
silent-failure class.

### A4 · `data.ts:916 getMostPlayedSongs` vs `data.ts:567 derivedSongList('frequent')` — **still present**

Two independent implementations of "compose most-played from the 15 most-frequent
albums, sort by `playCount`, fall back to album order": `data.ts:916` delegates to
`Subsonic.getMostPlayedSongs` (`subsonic.ts:1151`, which itself does
`getAlbumList(auth,'frequent',15,0)` + flatten + `playCount` sort + the no-counts
fallback at `:1161–1165`), while `derivedSongList` (`:567`, `DERIVED_POOL = 15`) does
the same thing through the query cache and the library merge. Which screen gets which
is invisible from the call sites (`:633` routes the Songs screen to the derived one).
Keep the `data.ts` one; delete the `subsonic.ts` export and its caller.
Impact ~35 lines.

### A5 · `data.ts:1334 flushOfflineQueue(auth: Subsonic.SubsonicAuth)` shadows `auth()` — **still present**

`data.ts:40 function auth()` (module helper) vs the parameter named `auth` at `:1334`;
inside that function `auth` means a profile value and 100 lines earlier in the same
file it means a call. `store/auth.ts:363` already names the same thing `profile`.
One-word fix, ~1 line, but it is the reason the queue-reconnect path is hard to read.

### A6 · `data.ts:208 export function coverArtUrl(id, _size?)` — *still present*

The parameter is underscore-named yet forwarded at `:208`/`:210`, and `songCoverUrl`
(`:236`) forwards `size?: number` un-defaulted, so any caller that omits it reaches
`subsonic.ts:1754`'s `size = 300` — a fourth tier the doc at `subsonic.ts:1734–1748`
explicitly says does not exist ("three of them on purpose… The app used to ask for
eight"). Verified across `src/app` + `src/components`: all 17 `songCoverUrl(` call
sites and all 48 `coverArtUrl(` call sites pass a `COVER.*` tier today (16× `card`,
3× `full`, 29× `thumb`), so `= 300` is a live default that no caller uses and every
future caller will accidentally adopt. Fix: `size: 0 | number` required, or default to `COVER.card` and
delete the `= 300`; impact ~4 lines but it closes a silent-cache-splitting trap
(one album, four cached copies).

## `src/api/subsonic.ts` (1968 lines)

### U1 · `subsonic.ts:982 reorderPlaylist` and `:1528 savePlayQueue` bypass `request()` — **still present, bug**

Both need a POST body (long id lists) so they re-implement the transport inline:

```ts
982   const res = await fetch(`${auth.serverUrl}/rest/createPlaylist.view`, { method: 'POST', … });
987   if (!res.ok) throw new Error(`Error de red (${res.status})`);
990   if (sub?.status === 'failed') throw new Error(sub.error?.message ?? 'Error de Subsonic');
```
Three things `request()` already does and these do not:
1. **No timeout.** `request()` wraps every GET in an `AbortController` +
   `REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS` (`:546–566`). These POSTs have no `signal` at all, so a
   non-answering server leaves a promise that never settles. The file's own header
   comment (`:9–14`) exists to explain that class ("React Native's stops delivering
   answers once the app is in the background: measured at eleven requests sent and
   none resolved… a worker that waits for it forever"), and `savePlayQueue` is the
   one call that runs *continuously in the background* (player.ts's periodic sync).
2. **Wrong error type.** Plain `Error`, in Spanish, untranslated (`Error de red (502)`,
   `Error de Subsonic`). Callers that branch on
   `e instanceof SubsonicRequestError && e.network` (`store/auth.ts:341`) take the wrong
   branch for a dead server, and `ErrorBoundary.tsx:28` renders the Spanish literal into
   any locale.
3. **No migration retry.** `request()`'s `code === ERR_NOT_FOUND` path (`:590–593`)
   retries with `canonicalId(id)`; these skip it, so reordering a playlist against a
   migrated Navidrome library fails permanently while everything else self-heals.

Cleaner shape — one POST sibling that keeps the retry/parse/error contract:
```ts
async function requestPost(auth, endpoint, params: URLSearchParams, allowOffline = false) {
  assertCanRequest(allowOffline);
  const signal = withTimeout(REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS);            // shared with request()
  netTally(endpoint, params.toString().length);
  const res = await fetch(`${auth.serverUrl}/rest/${endpoint}`, {
    method: 'POST', headers: { 'Content-Type': … }, body: params.toString(), signal });
  return unwrapSubsonic(await res.json());                  // the :586–596 block, extracted
}
```
`savePlayQueue` then also gets to report failure instead of swallowing it
(`:1533–1535` `catch {}` — the prior doc's #126 complaint, still present).
Impact ~90 lines rewritten; severity: **bug**.

### U2 · error-message conventions disagree across the API layer — **still present**

`subsonic.ts:560` `'Server took too long to respond'`, `:562` `'Could not connect to the
server'`, `:587` `'Unexpected server response'`, `:1891` `'The server did not return a
link'` — bare English, and the module never imports `tg`. `jellyfin.ts:214`/`:216` wrap
**the identical strings** in `tg(…)`. `navidrome.ts:52`/`:65`/`:96`/`:175`/`:336`
hardcode Spanish (`'Sin contraseña guardada'`, `'No se pudo conectar con el servidor'` ×5).
All reach the UI verbatim. One `apiError(key, network?, code?)` helper returning a
`SubsonicRequestError` and importing `tg`; delete the Spanish literals. Impact ~40.

### U3 · `subsonic.ts:1857 hasJukeboxRole` vs `:1903 hasShareRole` — same lookup, opposite contracts — **still present**

Both do `request<{ user?: … }>(auth, 'getUser.view', { username: auth.username })`.
`hasJukeboxRole` swallows everything (`:1863 catch { return false; }`); `hasShareRole`
deliberately rethrows network failures (`:1918 if ((e as {network?})?.network) throw e;`)
with an eight-line comment explaining that a cached "you cannot share" outlives the
hiccup that caused it by hours. That reasoning is correct for *both* roles —
`hasJukeboxRole` has the same bug the comment describes, answered by returning `false`.
Cleaner shape: one `getUser(auth): Promise<UserRole>` (throwing once, documented) plus
`role('jukeboxRole')`/`role('shareRole')` readers with a three-valued `boolean | null`
so callers cannot mistake "no answer" for "no permission" — the same three-valued
contract `serverAtLeast` (`:657`) already sets out to justify at length. Impact ~25.

### U4 · `subsonic.ts:1687 createRadioStation` and `:925 createPlaylist` — the same "the API doesn't return the id" workaround — **still present**

Both create, then re-list and search for the thing they made:
`createPlaylist` does `lists.find((p) => p.name === name)` (`:939`) — **first** name match;
`createRadioStation` does `[...stations].reverse().find(name && streamUrl)` (`:1702`) — **last**
match, and its comment (`:1700`) says why: "if there are name+URL duplicates, the last one
is the one we just created". So the more careful of the two searches is in the file twice,
and `createPlaylist` can return a *pre-existing* playlist's id when the user names a list
after one they already have — the user then adds songs to the wrong playlist, silently.
Also `createPlaylist`'s fallback re-runs `getPlaylists` (`:938`), whose Ampache filter
(`:894`, drops `owner === 'System'`) is applied to the lookup, so a created smartlist
is never found and `throw new Error('Created playlist not found')` (`:940`) is the
failure mode there. One `findByIdNewestFirst(auth, list, predicate)` with the reverse-scan
rule, and the two callsites reduced to it. Impact ~30, severity: bug (name collision).

### U5 · `subsonic.ts:470 SubsonicRequestError` carries `network` + `code` but nothing carries the HTTP status — *new-ish, small*

`reorderPlaylist` is the only place that ever sees a status (`res.status`) and it puts
it in a message string (`Error de red (${res.status})`). `TransferError` in
`downloads.ts` already has `status` as a field. Worth fixing with U1: `status?: number`
on the error class and `retryWithCanonicalId` gets to distinguish "not found from the
server" from "not found by the proxy" one day. Impact ~5.

### U6 · `subsonic.ts` dead export `ERR_NOT_FOUND` (`:482`) — **still present, verified repo-wide**

`grep -rn "\bERR_NOT_FOUND\b" src` matches only `subsonic.ts` itself (definition +
`:590` + `:853`). Same check for `naturalSongDir` (`navidrome.ts:214`),
`naturalAlbumDir` (`:222`), `TABLET_WIDTH` (`useScreenSize.ts:41`),
`GENRE_ART_COUNT` (`useGenreArt.ts:15`) — all four still exported and read nowhere else.
Drop the `export` keyword. Impact 5 lines.

### U7 · `subsonic.ts:1109 getAppearsOn` — unbounded approximation with a magic 200 — *still present*

`search3` with `songCount: 200`, then a per-song pass. The comment at `:1093` explains
why it is approximated (Subsonic has no endpoint) but nothing says why 200, and the
result is not paginated, so an artist with more than 200 matching songs silently loses
participation albums — and this is not one of `AGENT_ENDPOINTS` (`:454`), despite being
a heavy `search3` that Navidrome may answer by going out to the internet first. Impact
~5, severity: med (silent truncation).

### U8 · `subsonic.ts:215–221 streamReachable` — good abstraction, not used everywhere — *new since merge*

The doc comment claims "The one rule the rows, the queue marking and the player's
source decision all read" it. Verified callers: `TrackRow.tsx:168`, `player.ts:334`,
`player.ts:448`, `data.ts:262`. Verified non-callers reimplementing it:
`player.tsx:977` (`song.url && !song.vod`), `player.ts:858`/`:887`/`:2175`/`:3994`
(`song.url && !song.vod`), `player.tsx:1044` (`(!song.url || song.vod)` for speed).
The comment is currently a half-true claim about the codebase; see P5 above.

---

## `src/lib/localQueries.ts` (1379 lines) — unchanged by the merge (±0)

### L1 · tier cascade hand-copied 22× — **still present** (exact line numbers re-verified)

`grep -c "Falls through to the catalog in memory"` on the current file: **22** —
at `:403, :415, :483, :513, :536, :582, :590, :640, :659, :717, :757, :771, :823,
:848, :874, :982, :1153, :1268, :1324, :1332, :1355, :1363`. Every one is
`} catch { // Falls through to the catalog in memory. }` — an empty handler that
records nothing. Consequences: a corrupt downloads DB answers with the in-memory
catalog and nobody can tell; and the same corruption is invisible in diagnostics,
even though `diagnosticsReport.ts` has a channel for it.

Cleaner shape — one resolver that owns the fall-through and the note:
```ts
type Tier = { name: string; ask: () => T | null | undefined };
async function tiers(label: string, ...tiers: Tier[]): T | null {
  for (const t of tiers) {
    try { const v = await t.ask(); if (hasResult(v)) return v; }
    catch (e) { noteTierFailure(label, t.name, e); }   // observable, once
  }
  return null;
}
const rows = await tiers('albums page',
  () => dir && Cat.albumsPage(dir, order, size, offset),
  () => src && LocalCat.albumsPage(src, local, size, offset),
  () => inMemoryCatalog(),
);
```
Impact: removes ~1/3 of the file's body (the ~6-line try/catch wrapper × 22 ≈ 130 lines
plus the per-function `dir`/`src` preamble × 12 ≈ 120 lines) and makes the failure
class debuggable. Severity: high.

### L2 · the same ordering vocabulary encoded three times inside one function — **still present**

`getAlbumList` (`:388–472`, 85 lines): a downloads-DB lookup table inline at `:394`
(`{newest, random, alphabeticalByArtist}`), an `inMemory` boolean predicate at `:397`
(`type === 'recent' || 'frequent' || 'byYear'`), a *second* lookup table `LOCAL_ALBUM_ORDER`
(`:331`, a different vocabulary: `{newest, byYear, random, alphabeticalByArtist,
alphabeticalByName}`), and a *third* encoding — a `switch (type)` over all seven orders
at `:421–470`. `getSongList` (`:742–814`) repeats the same three-table shape with the
`SongListSort` vocabulary.

Net effect besides size: `byYear` is in the memory `switch` but not in the downloads-DB
table, so for a **server account offline** (the DB path) `getAlbumList('byYear')` skips
both DB tiers (`:398 if (dir && (order || !inMemory))`) and re-sorts the whole catalog
in JS on **every page** (`:471 albums.slice(offset, offset + size)` after a full
`.sort()`). On the "fifteen thousand songs" library the file's own comments cite
(`:232–236`), that is a 15k-album sort per page request, plus `getSongList`'s
15k-song sort per page (`:813`). Fix: one order table per source, derived from one
vocabulary — and have the memory fallback sort once per (source, order) rather than
per call. Impact ~120 lines + a real perf win. Severity: high.

### L3 · memory-search sentinel bug — **still present, bug**

`search`'s DB branch builds artist-hit keys with the catalog's sentinel
(`:1233 const withAlbumHit = named.map((a) => normKey(a.artist || UNKNOWN_ARTIST))`),
but the memory branch builds them without it:

```ts
1283  const artistsWithSongHit = new Set(titleHits.map((s) => normKey(s.artist || '')));
1294  const artistsWithAlbumHit = new Set(c.albums.filter(…).map((a) => normKey(a.artist || '')));
```
Artist ids in this layer are `normKey(artist || UNKNOWN_ARTIST)` — `toAlbum` at `:309`
does exactly that, and `getAppearsOn` (`:714`, `:728`) compares against
`normKey(x.artist || UNKNOWN_ARTIST)`. So in the local-profile branch an unknown-artist
song contributes the key `normKey('')` to a set that is later tested with
`artistsWithSongHit.has(a.id)` where `a.id === normKey(UNKNOWN_ARTIST)` — never true.
Tier 3 ("artist that has a matching song") silently never fires for the Unknown Artist
bucket offline. Impact ~10 lines (pass the sentinel), severity: **bug**.

### L4 · `localQueries.ts:1090 updatePlaylist` silently drops `public` — **still present, but Info (corrected 2026-10-22)**

Signature accepts `{ name?, comment?, public? }`; the spread at `:1099–1101` applies only
`name` and `comment`. `data.ts:1763–1770` builds the offline-queue patch *including*
`public`, and `data.ts:1776` forwards it to the server online. So an offline "make this
playlist public" is queued, replayed through this local function, and lost. Either
apply it (the local record has no such field, so: store it and surface it in `toPlaylist`
at `:954`) or reject it explicitly with a comment. Impact ~5.

**Corrected 2026-10-22 — nothing is lost, and the severity is Info, not bug.** A server
account never reaches this function: the patch is queued (`data.ts:1774`) and replayed to
the server *with* `public` (`data.ts:1461–1470`/`:1474–1480` → `subsonic.ts:952–964`). The
local-files profile does reach it (`data.ts:1767`) — and the toggle that could send a
changed `public` is hidden there (`app/playlist/[id].tsx:622 hidePublic={offline}`).
Dead parameter, no user-visible defect; fix = drop it from the local signature or comment
it. Not shipped; close with the local-playlist cluster.

### L5 · `localQueries.ts:1119 newestFirst` and `:1141 inOrder` — two orderings of one thing — **still present**

`newestFirst(ids, items, id)` at `:1119` and, 22 lines later inside `getStarred`,
`const inOrder = <T>(ids, rows) => ids.slice().reverse().map(id => rows.get(id)).filter(…)`.
Same rule, one takes an array, one takes a Map. Keep `newestFirst` over a Map-backed
accessor. Impact ~12.

### L6 · `getAlbum` (`:491–570`) and `getArtist` (`:598–685`) — one fallback rule written three times with three answers — **still present**

`getAlbum`'s "no catalog entry" album name: DB path uses `first.album ?? ''` (`:504`),
local path uses `songs[0]?.album || tg('Unknown album')` (`:529`), memory path uses the
same as local (`:565`). Offline with a shelf-less album, the DB path yields an **empty
title** where the other two give the translated placeholder.
`getArtist`'s name: DB path `name ?? rows[0]?.artist ?? ''` (`:630`), local path
`albums[0]?.artist || artistId` (`:654`), memory path
`albums[0]?.artist || (useAuthStore.getState().auth ? '' : artistId)` (`:680`).
Three spellings of "prefer the real name, then a placeholder, then maybe the raw id",
with the raw-id case deliberately allowed only in local mode (the comment at `:668–679`
says why: `ar-3f9a…` printed as someone's name). That nuance is exactly what belongs in
a helper: `fallbackName(kind, albumRows, artistRows, isLocalProfile)`. Impact ~30,
severity: med-high (visible gibberish/blank titles offline).

### L7 · `serverArtistId` (`:695`) / `getArtist`'s DB branch (`:601–641`) — one round trip, then five — **still present**

The DB branch does `artistByServerId` → `getDownloadShelf()` → `artistAlbums` →
`artistFromSongs` → `albumsByIds`, each awaited separately. Worse, `albumsByIds`
(`downloadsDb.ts`) is **not chunked**:
```ts
const marks = ids.map(() => '?').join(',');   // one IN (…) with every id
```
while its sibling `songsByIds` chunks at a hardcoded `400` (`downloadsDb.ts:594`)
despite `PARAM_CHUNK = 400` existing at `downloadsDb.ts:315` (and `localDb.ts:479`
using it properly at `:485`). An artist with >~1000 downloaded albums throws a
SQLite parameter error, which lands in the `catch` at `:640` → "no albums", i.e. the
artist screen answers empty. Reachable from a reviewed file: `localQueries.ts:621`.
Fix: export `PARAM_CHUNK` once and use it in all five DB modules
(prior doc's claim — still present), and chunk `albumsByIds`. Impact ~10, severity: **bug**.
  - **Severity corrected 2026-10-22 (measured):** ">~1000" is off by ~32×. expo-sqlite vendors SQLite with `#define SQLITE_MAX_VARIABLE_NUMBER 32766` (`node_modules/expo-sqlite/vendor/sqlite3/sqlite3.c:14262`) and a probe of a stock sqlite3 build accepted 30,000 placeholders in this exact query shape, so the empty artist screen needs ≈32k album ids; on Android/iOS the limit is unverified (`libsql` prebuilt). **Latent — not shipped as its own PR**; close it with the DB-layer merge, and note that the chunk has to re-sort across chunks (`ORDER BY added_at DESC, name COLLATE NOCASE` is SQL-side today) and reproduce SQLite's ASCII-only `NOCASE` fold.

### L8 · module-scope caches with hand-tagged profile keys — **still present**

`favCache`/`favCacheKey` (`:51`/`:52`), `playlistCache`/`playlistCacheKey` (`:909`/`:910`),
`loadingPromise: Promise<any>` (`:148`) and `scanning` (`:252`). Two problems:
- `Promise<any>` at `:148` erases the shape; `ensureScanCatalog` returns
  `getLocalCatalog(mode, key)` which is `… | undefined`, so callers of
  `ensureScanCatalog()` (`rescan()` at `:296`) get an unchecked `undefined`.
- `loadFavs`/`saveFavs` (`:54`/`:71`) and `loadPlaylists`/`savePlaylists`
  (`:912`/`:937`) are the same cache-tagging dance twice.
One `profileCache<T>(key: () => string, load: (k) => T)` (the prior doc's request) deletes
all four variable pairs; impact ~80 lines. Severity: med.

### L9 · `rescan()` (`:288`) and `resetLocalLoading()` (`:125`) mutate `loadingPromise`/`scanning` from outside their owners — *still present*

`resetLocalLoading()` sets both to `null` without cancelling the scan already running,
so the abandoned scan's result still lands and `queryClient.invalidateQueries()` still
fires for a catalog that is now stale-by-source-change. The doc comment at `:120–124`
describes the intent ("changing folders mid-scan would wait for the old one and then
look for a catalog under the new key, which nobody built") but the code only forgets the
*handle*, not the work. An `AbortController` threaded into `ensureScanned`/`loadFolderSongs`
is the honest fix. Impact ~15, severity: med (wasted work + a spurious invalidate, and
`ensureScanCatalog`'s `finally` at `:194` already nulls the handle, so the two owners
fight each other).

## `src/components/TrackListView.tsx` (1546 lines) — merge touched only 11 lines

### T1 · 1039-line component with 35 props (`:229`) — **still present**

`Props` is `:109–227` (119 lines, **35 optional/required props**); the component body is
`:229–1268` (1039 lines) of which the `return` block is `:695–1268` (574 lines of JSX).
The file has no sub-component boundary anywhere between `:229` and `:1268` — the header,
the search bar, the two play buttons, the genre chips, the action row and the fixed top
bar are all inline in one expression.

Splitting it the way the props already imply deletes the "one 1000-line component"
problem outright:
```tsx
<TrackListView …>                      // list only: rows, paging, filter, selection
  <Header slot={…}>…</Header>          // cover + gradient + title + meta + genres + actions
</TrackListView>
```
i.e. `Header` becomes a component taking `(cover, gradient, title, subtitle, meta,
description, genres, actions, scroll)` — the ~140 `ListHeaderComponent` lines at
`:801–1119` move out, `playOverlay` (`:672–693`) becomes a `DockedPlayButton` used twice,
and the search bar (`:317–402` state + `:803–839` JSX + `:1298–1331` styles) becomes
`SearchableInList`, which is already the third copy of that box in the repo
(prior doc's `TrackListView.tsx:800–823` / `PlaylistsBrowser.tsx:177–201` /
`PodcastAddSheet.tsx:97–113`, **still present** — verified: `searchBox` height 44 at
`:1320` with `Icon name="search" size={18}` + `TextInput` + `close-circle` "Clear"
Pressable at `:809–830`). Impact ~250.

### T2 · four parallel index spaces, threaded by hand — **still present**

Every row lives in four coordinate systems and the file re-derives the mapping at
six sites:
1. position in the **shown** list (the FlatList's `index`),
2. position in the **original** `songs` array (`origIndex`),
3. the **server** index (`playlistIndices[i]`),
4. the **row key** (`rowKeys[i]` = `id#n`).

Sites: `rowKeys` built at `:418–425`; `filtered` carries the original index at
`:634–643`; `keyExtractor` `:768` re-derives with
`rowKeys[filtered ? filtered[index].index : index]`; `renderItem` re-derives again at
`:1124` (`const origIndex = filtered ? filtered[index].index : index`), then uses it for
`:1127` (`discHeaders?.[origIndex]`), `:1133` (`position`), `:1139`
(`playlistIndices[origIndex]`), `:1143`/`:1160` (`rowKey`), and `runSelectionAction`
at `:447–458` walks a *fifth* combination (`selectedIds?.has(rowKeys[i])` with
`indices.push(playlistIndices ? playlistIndices[i] : i)`).

This is the classic place where an off-by-one or a stale key becomes a wrong-row bug —
and the file itself records that it happened twice: `#132` (the `id#n` key fix) and the
comment at `:766` ("the same song can be in the list twice and React was being handed
the same key for both"). Cleaner shape — one row model built once, so the FlatList data
*is* the row:
```ts
type Row = { song: Song; origIndex: number; serverIndex: number; key: string };
const rows: Row[] = useMemo(() => { …build once, filter once… }, [songs, query, playlistIndices]);
// data = rows, keyExtractor = (_, i) => rows[i].key,
// renderItem = ({ item, index }) => row = rows[index]; // no re-derivation anywhere
```
Impact ~40 lines and it deletes the class of "did I mean shown index or original index"
bugs. Severity: high.

### T3 · the play button is drawn twice with mirrored a11y attributes — **still present**

Two complete `Pressable` trees, `slotOpacity`/`dockOpacity` swapping them at one
scroll threshold (`:611–620` interpolations, `:622–630` and `:794–798` state flips):

- header copy `:1076–1096` (`playBtnRef`, `slotOpacity`,
  `accessibilityElementsHidden={overBar && !selecting}`, `onPress` → `onPlay(0)`),
- overlay copy `:672–693` (`dockOpacity`,
  `pointerEvents={overBar && !selecting ? 'auto' : 'none'}`,
  `accessibilityElementsHidden={!overBar || selecting}`, `onPress` → `onPlay(0)`).

The two blocks are the same button with the same label, the same icon, the same
`styles.playButton`, and **inverted** a11y props. Getting one of the four pairs wrong
is an invisible-but-tappable or announced-but-untappable button, and there is no place
in the file where the invariant "exactly one of these is exposed" is stated once.
Cleaner shape: `PlayButton({ at: 'header' | 'dock', active, onPress })` where
`active` is computed once (`overBar && !selecting`) and the component owns the whole
prop set; the two call sites then read `<PlayButton at="header" active={!overBar} …/>`
and `<PlayButton at="dock" active={overBar} …/>`. Impact ~40. Severity: high
(a11y drift is a real user-facing risk, plus `PLAY_SIZE` is the 56px re-declaration the
prior doc lists at `BrowseToolbar.tsx:170–173` / `TrackListSkeleton.tsx:89`).

### T4 · two owners of `overBar`, one of them with no dependency list — **still present**

`setOverBar` is driven from three places: the measuring `useLayoutEffect` at `:623–630`,
the scroll listener at `:794–798`, and the initial `useState(false)` at `:346`, with
`overBarRef` (`:347`) as a third copy of the same boolean. Same three-line dance twice
(`should !== overBarRef.current → overBarRef.current = should; setOverBar(should)`).
Extract `const setOverBarIfChanged = (y) => {…}` used by both. Impact ~10, severity:
med (a state machine with three owners is what makes the drift class here unreviewable).

### T5 · `useLayoutEffect` at `:542–578` has **no dependency array** → two `measureInWindow` round trips on every render — **still present**

Bare `useLayoutEffect(() => {…})` re-runs on *every* render of the component. The body
does two `measureInWindow` calls (`:566`, `:572`) — native layout queries — plus a
`requestAnimationFrame(() => setMeasureTick(…))` self-bounce (`:558`) whose only purpose
is to force *another* render. Combined with the comment at `:350–354`
("Only the write is used: the effect reads no dependencies, any render will do, and this
is what makes one happen") and `:539–541` ("Measured after every render … and again when
the search bar's animation ends"), the file has engineered an effect→setState→render→
effect loop on the layout path of a list screen.
Cleaner shape: keep the bare effect (it is what "after every render" means) but make it
cheap and explicit — a single `onLayout` on `headerRootRef` (RN's `onLayout` fires on
layout, not on every render) measuring one node, and read the button's offset from
constants, since the button's place in the header *is* laid out from constants
(`styles.playSlot` is `PLAY_SIZE` inside `styles.actions`, whose gaps are all tokens).
Impact ~40 lines replaced, severity: perf.

### T6 · invisible second `Text` drawn to count lines (`:936–968`) — **still present**

The description is laid out twice per render — an absolutely-positioned, `opacity: 0`
copy whose only job is `onTextLayout` line-counting (`:941–948`,
`styles.descriptionMeasure` `:1428–1436`), then the real one with `numberOfLines`
(`:949–954`). A text-shaping pass per duplicate on every render of a header with a
description. The extra copy exists because `onTextLayout` on the *visible* element only
reports the lines that survived — but the count can come from the same element plus a
measured per-line budget, or from `numberOfLines` + a chevron when the string exceeds a
cached width/line estimate. Impact ~30, severity: med.

### T7 · misleading local name: `const searchBar = !!searchable && songs.length > 0` (`:330`) — **still present**

`searchBar` is a boolean meaning "the box is drawable", while `styles.searchBar`
(`library.tsx:1524`) is a real style and `searchClip`/`searchRow`/`searchBox`/`searchInput`
are the JSX right next to it. Rename to `searchEnabled` / `canSearch`. Impact 1 line.

### T8 · `band = searchable && !wideCover ? SEARCH_H * 4 : 0` (`:509`) — magic multiplier — **still present**

`SEARCH_H * 4` (= 288) has no derivation while `SEARCH_H` (`:74`) is the box height and
`band` is the accent strip above the gradient (`:744–750`). `gradientTop = wideCover ?
wideH : -band` (`:510`) is the only contract: "cover the gap the revealed row leaves",
which is `SEARCH_H`, not `4×`. Impact ~5, severity: low-med.

### T9 · `OVERSCROLL_H = 1200` (`:83`) — screen-height assumption in a themed style — *still present*

Used at `:853–862` (`height: OVERSCROLL_H, marginTop: -OVERSCROLL_H`) with the comment
"Tall enough for any pull; one view, no paint cost at rest, where it hangs off screen."
That is only free while overscroll stays bounded; derive from `screenH`
(`useScreenSize()` is already in scope at `:268`). Impact ~4.

### T10 · the 35-prop `Props` interface leaks render decisions into the API — **med**

Props such as `wideCover: { height, render(width, height) => ReactNode }`,
`renderCover?: (size) => ReactNode`, `footer?: ReactNode`, `emptyState?: ReactNode`,
`addAction?: { icon?: keyof typeof Icon.glyphMap; … }` put layout and icon-choice
decisions in a shared component's signature (and `icon: keyof typeof Icon.glyphMap`
leaks the icon font's type into every caller). Cleaner shape: `Cover` variants become a
small discriminated union (`{ kind: 'server' | 'wide' | 'favoritesArt', height? }`),
and `addAction.icon` becomes a semantic token (`'add' | 'shuffle'`). Impact ~30,
severity: med (this is why the props list is unreviewable, not just long).

---

## `src/app/player.tsx` (1928 lines)

### V1 · 1465-line component — **still present**

`export default function PlayerScreen()` at `:265`, closed at `:1729` (the `styles`
block is `:1731–1928`). Its `return` block alone is `:1152–1728` (577 lines of JSX).
There is not one sub-component extracted between those lines — the carousel, the
controls cluster, the lyrics card, the info rows and the backdrop are all inline,
while the file *does* extract two tiny ones (`CircleButton` `:161`, `SleepButton` `:199`).
Cleaner shape: the same split TrackListView needs (T1) — one screen = one screen
component + `PlayerHeader` / `PlayerControls` / `LyricsPane` / `StarRow`, with the shared
layout state passed down as one `layout` object (see V2). Impact ~200.

### V2 · module-global layout cache with an import-time storage read — **still present**

```ts
135  let lastLayout: PlayerLayout | null = null;
138  const LAYOUT_KEY = 'resonus.playerLayout';
139  getItem(LAYOUT_KEY)
140    .then((raw) => {
141      if (!raw || lastLayout) return;
142      const v = JSON.parse(raw) as PlayerLayout;
143      if ([v.for, v.pageH, v.coverH, v.coverW, v.starsH].every((n) => typeof n === 'number')) {
144        lastLayout = v;
145      }
146    })
147    .catch(() => {});
```
Reads SecureStore-adjacent storage **at import time**, unawaited, and writes a module
global from inside the callback. The global is then mutated at `:616` (inside a
`useEffect` in the component), persisted at `:617`, and read at `:507` and `:531`.
Four problems:
1. **Module init side effect**: the read races the screen's own first render;
   the guard `if (!raw || lastLayout) return` (`:141`) is a hand-rolled "whoever got
   there first wins" that only works because both writers check the same global.
2. **Two player screens clobber each other**: the key is a module global, not per
   instance. `player.tsx:266–267` documents why a second mount exists at all
   ("a stack keeps this screen mounted while you are on another one"), so a stacked
   player overwrites the visible player's remembered layout on every layout write.
3. **Validation accepts nonsense**: `typeof n === 'number'` admits `0` and negatives,
   so a corrupt blob yields `pageH = 0`/`coverH = -800` which passes
   `pageH <= 0` guards by luck, not by contract.
4. **`JSON.stringify(next) === JSON.stringify(lastLayout)`** (`:615`) as a change test
   on a 5-field object — allocation + compare on a hot path, and the shape is
   re-serialised on every write (`:617`).
Cleaner shape: one `useMeasuredLayout(key, measure)` hook in `src/hooks` (the repo's
hook-per-concern pattern) owning `{for, …} + storage + validation`, returning a
`layoutRef` to the screen; the module global disappears, the import-time read becomes a
hook effect, and validation becomes `every(n => Number.isFinite(n) && n > 0)`.
Impact ~120. Severity: high.

### V3 · `spins` and `spinsSV` — one counter, two owners — **still present**

`:687 const [spins, setSpins] = useState(0)` and `:688 const spinsSV = useSharedValue(0)`,
with the comment at `:677–680` admitting it ("It is kept twice on purpose. `spins` is
React's, because it decides which song each panel shows; `spinsSV` is the gesture's").
Writes: `spinsSV.value -= advance` (`:724`), `spinsSV.value = base + advance` (`:771`),
reads at `:702`, `:747`, `:757`, `:894` (`paneRel(k)` over `spins`), `:724`. The two
counters can only be kept equal by discipline: `paneRel` at `:894` reads the React one
while the gesture writes the Reanimated one, so a swipe that commits without a
`setSpins` render moves the panels without changing which songs they show — and the
inverse desync is a song swap with static panels. Cleaner shape: one Reanimated
`SharedValue` plus a `useReducer`-fed React mirror derived from it through a
`useWatch`-style subscription (or make `spins` a `useState` written only inside the
native→JS hand-off, so the invariant is a single assignment). Impact ~40, severity:
med-high (silent desync class).

### V4 · orphaned doc comment — **still present**

The block at `:183–192` ("Position and opacity of a carousel panel (recycled). The 3
panels form an infinite strip…") documents `usePaneStyle` — which sits 58 lines away at
`:250`. It was left above `SleepButton` (`:199`), whose own doc comment (`:193–198`)
follows it, so the file currently reads as: carousel docs, then sleep-timer docs, then a
sleep-timer function, then (far below) the carousel function with a bare signature.
This is the single most misleading comment in the file: a reader editing the carousel
greps for "infinite strip" and lands on the sleep timer. Move it to `:249`. Impact 0
lines (relocate), severity: low but it actively misleads.

### V5 · `SWIPE_SHARE = 0.25` declared twice — **still present**

`player.tsx:120` and `MiniPlayer.tsx:44`, both used as `screenW * SWIPE_SHARE`
(`player.tsx:753`, `MiniPlayer.tsx:125`) for the same affordance (swipe to next/previous).
One token in `@/theme/motion` (e.g. `motion.swipeShare`). Impact 2 lines.

### V6 · `useSettings`/`usePlayerStore` reads and settings-driven layout inline in the screen — **still present**

The screen's layout math (`:493–608`) reads settings-driven rows (`canRate`, the
quality badge, album info, rating) and turns each into a measured slot height
subtracted from the cover (`starsH`, `coverBoxH`). This is the same "the cover is the
only elastic piece" machine the file documents at `:509–516`. It belongs in a
`usePlayerLayout(screenH, insets, options)` hook so the screen reads
`layout.coverH` instead of owning five `useState` slots, three `useEffect`s
(`:530–536`, `:596–608`, `:611–618`) and the `laidOut`/`coverStable` two-step reveal
machinery (`:527`, `:607`). Impact ~100 of V2's family; severity: high.

### V7 · podcast-episode favorite logic added in three places — **new since merge**

The merge put episode-heart logic in `player.tsx` at three sites: the favorite action
(`:799–810`, a new early-return branch inside a handler that then continues with the
server path), `favorited = heartedSong(song, podcastFav, favIds)` (`:956`) and
`podcastHeart = episodeHeart(song)` (`:958`), and two `<FavoriteButton … podcast={podcastHeart}>`
call sites (`:1327–1332`, `:1550–1555`). The early return at `:809` means the two
favorite paths now have two different error contracts (episode path toasts
`Couldn't complete the action` at `:807`; song path relies on
`applyStarChange`'s own reporting). The prior doc's finding "the confirm-delete
`Dialog` is duplicated / `toast(t("Couldn't complete the action"))` repeats 12×"
is the same class — the new code added a 13th. Cleaner shape: one
`toggleHeart(song, channel)` in `api/podcasts.ts` that returns
`{ added: boolean } | throws`, and one call site. Impact ~30. Severity: med.

### V8 · `sourceLabel` chain grew a `!song.vod` test that duplicates `streamReachable` — **new since merge**

`:977` now reads `song.url && !song.vod ? t('Radio')` while `subsonic.ts:219`
`streamReachable(song)` exists and is documented as "the one rule the rows, the
queue marking and the player's source decision all read". Verified non-use here
(see U8/P5). Impact ~2 lines + the rule count, severity: latent bug.

   descriptions are byte-identical in this respect to what the prior doc saw.




## Addendum — two more verified items (found while re-checking the prior doc)

10. **Duplicated hook-read header, still present at both screen roots** —
    `player.tsx:266–269` and `library.tsx:1134–1144` both open with the same
    three lines: the identical two-line comment ("Repaints on a change of
    appearance or accent: a stack keeps this screen mounted while you are on
    another one…"), then `useTheme();` and `useSettings((s) => s.appFont);
    // re-render when font changes`. Only these two screens are ever mounted
    as a *stack peer*, so the rule ("anything that can be mounted off-screen
    must repaint on appearance/font change") belongs in one place — a `useRepaint()`
    hook composing `useTheme()` + the font dep, ~4 lines and one future foot-gun
    (a third stacked screen that forgets it renders with the stale accent).

11. **`library.tsx:1346–1369` is a fourth hand-drawn search box** — `Icon name="search"
    size={18}` + `TextInput` + a `close-circle` "Clear" `Pressable hitSlop={10}`,
    styled by `searchRow`/`searchBar`/`searchInput` (`:1524`/`:1528` height 44/`:1534`).
    It is the same box as `TrackListView.tsx:809–830` (whose Clear `Pressable` is
    `hitSlop={8}`, `:823`, i.e. the same control with a *different* touch target),
    `PlaylistsBrowser.tsx:180`, `PodcastAddSheet.tsx:97`, `podcasts.tsx:202`,
    `radio.tsx:195`, `browse/songs.tsx:328`, `browse/albums.tsx:223`,
    `browse/artists.tsx:256`, `genres.tsx:100`, `favorites-add.tsx:175` —
    **eleven** copies repo-wide, four of them inside the reviewed files.
    One `SearchBox` prop (`{ query, onChange, onFocus, searchable, animated }`)
    deletes ~200 JSX + ~60 style lines and makes the 44-height, the `pill`/`md`
    radius comment and the Clear-button affordance single-sourced; the `hitSlop`
    drift is a live accessibility bug (42 px vs 52 px target for the same glyph).
